Honourable MPs were presented by the President on 17 October 2018 by the President of the Grand National Assembly of Turkey as the first budget of the presidential government system, the Central Management Budget Law Proposal of 2019. The central management budget funds for 2019 are estimated at 961 billion euros, budget revenues at $880.4 billion, budget deficits at 80.6 billion. These figures correspond to 16.9 per cent in budget costs, 17.4 per cent in budget revenues, and 11.8% in budget deficits, according to forecasts of the year 2018. The deficit, expected to be 1.9 per cent in 2018, is estimated to take place at 1.8 per cent in 2019. The Central Future Programme, which is the road map of the economy for the next period, was introduced as the New Economic Programme on September 20, 2018. The theme of this programme, which covered 2019-2021, was determined as balancing, discipline and change. The main aim of the programme was to restore price stability and financial stability in the short term, balancing the economy and maintaining budget discipline, implementing sustainable growth in the middle term and economic change in the interest of fair sharing. The macroeconomic goals set out with the new Economy Programme include some inconsistencies, but are generally realistic estimates. The goals in the new Economic Programme point to a strict budget policy. The budget aims to reduce public investments by 36.1 per cent, a total of 75.9 billion euros, including the savings of $59.9.9 billion and the increase of $16 billion. The economy envisions a significant slowdown of growth, with 3.8 per cent expected in 2018 expected growth estimated to drop to 2.3 per cent in 2019. The first source of economic growth is fixed capital investments; the second source is increasing efficiency across the country. More production, more exports, more jobs and more revenues need more investments, and the way to do that is to increase domestic savings first. Second, we use our own resources and resources from abroad in efficient, productive areas. Here, it is of great importance that public authority directs the country's allocation of resources.
